TASK ID: MISSION-TEAM
TASK NAME: Every mission is run by JAI's team: the Orchestrator plans, specialist sub-agents work in parallel, an integration lead builds and tests, the Verifier accepts
STATUS: COMPLETE
FILES CREATED: apps/backend/app/core/command/mission_org.py, apps/backend/tests/test_mission_team.py
FILES MODIFIED: apps/backend/app/core/command/agent_executor.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/service.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/continuation.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/tool_runtime.py, apps/backend/app/core/agents/toolbox.py, apps/backend/app/core/config.py, apps/backend/app/core/ops/docker_ops.py, apps/backend/app/core/voice/live.py, apps/backend/tests/conftest.py, apps/backend/tests/test_service_switches.py
COMMANDS RUN: python3 -m compileall on every changed file; backend test suite; backend and worker restarted
TESTS RUN: backend suite with new tests: a mission planned into two packages runs a Senior Software Engineer and a Technical Writer at the same time, each refused a write outside its own paths, neither offered delete or push, every sub-agent recorded on the Swarm page with its parent agent and dissolved at the end, then an integration lead builds and the Verifier accepts on evidence for both requirements; a one-change plan runs as one agent with no swarm; the plan is cleaned (unknown specialists, paths outside the workspace, unknown dependencies, a requirement given twice) and runs in dependency waves with no two packages writing the same paths; dev servers and watchers are not run; a stopped command takes everything it started with it; a hung tool and a model that stops answering are stopped by the watchdog; switching a service off waits for it to close down
RESULT: Missions ran as one agent with 40 steps, however big the job, while JAI's specialist agents and swarm sat unused, so MYCO Phase 0 crawled for two days. Now the Orchestrator plans each mission from its requirements, the workspace and the specification into up to 10 work packages, each for one of 21 specialists briefed with that role's working standard; the Swarm Manager spawns them as sub-agents (authority and tools no more than the lead's, no deleting or pushing, no further spawning), up to 4 at once in dependency waves, each writing only in the paths its package owns while commands, installs and git take turns on the machine; every sub-agent, task and tool call is on the Swarm page with its parent agent and cost. The integration lead then builds and tests the whole and fixes the joins, and the independent Verifier accepts only on evidence for every requirement; a later round plans again for what is still open. A step can no longer hang a mission: a model call that does not answer in 15 minutes (twice) ends the round with the work kept, a tool that does not return in 20 minutes is stopped, a dev server or watcher (npm run dev, vite, uvicorn, compose without -d) is not started, and a timed-out command's whole process group is stopped. Paths copied out of a sentence lose its brackets and punctuation. Switching the video maker or image generator off no longer reports a failure while it closes down (the request waited 10 seconds for a 20-second stop). The voice's mission updates include who on the team is doing what.

The sandbox sees only the workspaces folder (no Docker socket, database or provider key) and the agent's shell runs as an unprivileged user without the tokens. With the sandbox on, every mission with a workspace is done by specialists (one Senior Software Engineer for a small one) and finished by an integration engineer who builds and tests the whole, then the lead confirms and the Verifier decides; with it off, the team works as before. TASK ID: SANDBOX-ENGINEERING-RULES
TASK NAME: Sandbox specialists install the tools a project pins themselves, do fresh-clone checks in /tmp, and a run lost to a sandbox restart is reported at once
STATUS: COMPLETE
FILES CREATED: None
FILES MODIFIED: services/openhands/app/server.py, services/openhands/tests/test_sandbox.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_engine.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_policy.py, apps/backend/tests/test_openhands_sandbox.py
COMMANDS RUN: sandbox image build and its tests (no network); backend test suite; sandbox rebuilt, backend and worker restarted
TESTS RUN: sandbox suite: the conversation's system prompt carries the specialist's standard and JAI's engineering rules; backend suite: specialists' briefs say to install pinned tools themselves and to clone into /tmp; a fresh-clone check (git clone into /tmp, task bootstrap and task verify there) and "mise install syft@1.52.0" are allowed while other projects stay refused from /tmp; a run lost to a sandbox restart is reported at once as lost
RESULT: The sandbox mission's specialists did real work on MYCO Phase 0 (lint, type-check, build, unit, integration, e2e and Terraform tests passing; audit, verification report and checkpoint evidence written) but stopped at Syft: MYCO pins Syft 1.52.0, it was not installed, and they reported it as an action for Richard instead of installing it. Their earlier fresh-clone checks had also been cloned inside the repository, leaving .clone-verification folders that break the Biome formatter, and a fresh clone in /tmp would have been refused because the terminal left the workspace. Every sandbox specialist now works to JAI's engineering rules: install a tool the project pins at its exact version (mise install tool@version, then mise exec), never treat a missing tool as Richard's; clone into /tmp for fresh-clone checks and remove leftover clone folders; run checks to the end. /tmp is the sandbox's scratch space (other projects stay refused). A run lost because the sandbox was restarted is reported at once, and the next round carries on from the files on disk.
BLOCKERS: None
NEXT TASK: MYCO Phase 0

TASK ID: SANDBOX-WHOLE-MISSION
TASK NAME: Every sandbox mission prepares its environment first, uses all of JAI's knowledge and capabilities, is checked by a Verifier with a full shell, and an upgrade is kept only after a real mission passes
STATUS: COMPLETE
FILES CREATED: apps/backend/app/api/v1/agent_tools.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/acceptance.py
FILES MODIFIED: apps/backend/app/core/command/mission_org.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_engine.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_policy.py, apps/backend/app/api/v1/agent_gate.py, apps/backend/app/main.py, apps/backend/tests/test_openhands_sandbox.py, services/openhands/app/server.py, services/openhands/tests/test_sandbox.py
COMMANDS RUN: sandbox image build and its tests (no network); backend test suite; sandbox rebuilt, backend and worker restarted; the acceptance mission (a real mission on a sample project, with JAI's model) and a fresh-clone run of its acceptance command
TESTS RUN: backend: a sandbox mission runs the environment engineer first, the specialists, the integration engineer and the independent Verifier, all in the sandbox (no step in JAI's old tool loop), and is accepted only on the Verifier's evidence for every requirement; a requirement the Verifier did not judge is unmet; a package's paths are guidance, not a block; moving with cd .. inside the workspace is allowed; the Verifier may look and test (fresh clone in /tmp included) but never change files; JAI's MCP tool server offers memory, knowledge, research, documents and the rest, not file or command tools, refuses runs JAI did not register, keeps the Verifier from adding, and records each call. Sandbox: the conversation is configured with JAI's MCP server and its rules name JAI's knowledge tools. Installer: a whole real mission on a sample project must finish verified and pass its acceptance command in a fresh clone before the upgrade is kept
RESULT: Three days on MYCO Phase 0 came from JAI being held back rather than from the work: the lead and the Verifier still used JAI's old tool loop (one plain command, no /tmp), so the specification's fresh-clone acceptance check could never be run and Phase 0 could never be accepted; specialists were blocked from files outside their package; nothing prepared the environment, so a pinned tool missing (Syft 1.52.0) stopped every package; and the sandbox's specialists had none of JAI's knowledge or services. Now a sandbox mission is: the Environment Engineer (every pinned tool at its version, bootstrap passing), the planned specialists, the integration engineer (the whole build, tests and acceptance commands), and the independent Verifier, which runs the acceptance commands itself with a full shell (fresh clone in /tmp) and never changes files; its evidence for every requirement, with JAI's hard checks, decides the round. Every specialist has JAI's memory, knowledge graph, Richard's wishes, specialist knowledge, research, documents, browser, code understanding and media through JAI's MCP server, each call through JAI's toolbox at the run's authority and recorded. Only Richard's hard boundaries stay blocked. Each upgrade is now proved by a real mission before it is kept.

NEXT TASK: MYCO Phase 0

TASK ID: TEAM-BUDGET-FIX
TASK NAME: Specialists are no longer stopped at a fifth of their budget; a dropped connection or a spent package no longer ends the work; a new mission carries on from the last one
STATUS: COMPLETE
FILES CREATED: apps/backend/app/core/command/resume.py, apps/backend/tests/test_mission_resume.py
FILES MODIFIED: apps/backend/app/core/command/service.py, services/openhands/app/server.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_engine.py, scripts/scan-skill-packs.sh, services/openhands/tests/test_sandbox.py, apps/backend/tests/test_openhands_sandbox.py
COMMANDS RUN: sandbox and backend test suites; sandbox, backend and worker recreated; AgentShield scan of every skill pack again; the read-only mission diagnostic
TESTS RUN: LiteLLM prices a call with 90% cached input at about $0.10 instead of $0.42 once the cache prices are registered; the sandbox is sent the cache read and write prices; a run lost to "Anthropic could not be reached" waits, runs again from the files on disk, and finishes on GPT-6 Sol if Anthropic is still unreachable; a package that hits its spending limit gets one more run and never loops; a mission that says to carry on, or names the same phase as the last unfinished mission in its workspace, takes over its requirements (done ones keep their evidence, the rest are open) and an account of where it left off, while new work or work after a verified mission starts fresh
RESULT: The 8 November missions failed because every specialist was stopped by "Agent reached maximum budget limit ($6.0000)": the sandbox gave LiteLLM a flat per-token price, which bills every cached token at the full input price, so an agent loop's spend was counted about five times too high and specialists stopped at roughly $1.20 of real spend. The sandbox now registers Opus 5.5's and Sol's full price lists (cache reads at a tenth), so the $6 limit means $6. A momentary Anthropic outage (12:55) no longer ends a package, and a package that does spend its limit is continued once from its own files. AgentShield's report is now written to a file, so ECC's large report is no longer cut off at 64 KB. Real spending stays capped by the daily limit at the gateway. A new mission used to derive a fresh requirement list, so "carry on with Phase 0" re-planned finished work; it now carries on from the last mission's state, and the Verifier still checks every requirement, carried-over ones included, against the files.
BLOCKERS: None
NEXT TASK: Read the mission record (planning, parallel waves) and restart MYCO Phase 0 and the Plexecute 2 video

STATUS: COMPLETE

COMMANDS RUN: sandbox image build and tests; backend test suite on PostgreSQL; sandbox, backend, worker and scheduler recreated
TESTS RUN: by default one Lead Builder then the Verifier (no planner, environment or integration stage); parallel writers only when the brief asks, after the builder's skeleton; a one-skill job is one specialist, BRAG on Opus at high effort with 200 steps and a $10 allowance, the Verifier judging contact sheets; Sonnet 5.5 for writers, Opus for the builder, the Verifier and takeovers, a stopped writer taken over once by Opus; Anthropic's prices for Sonnet and Opus (cache hits $0.10 and $0.20); each mission's own allowance stops it with its progress kept and gives each package only its share; an interrupted round keeps its finished packages; a late heartbeat with live progress is never re-queued; rounds that complete no requirement stop the mission; the phase's frozen ledger becomes the requirements and its blocked items are reported with their failure kind; jai-check prints only failures and reuses an unchanged pass; the model sees the images it opens; the approvals digest groups what waits by kind; repair actions run in shadow until promoted, never for postgres, redis or the Docker proxy; sales email only to businesses shown to be a company or LLP, or with consent Richard recorded
RESULT: The 8 November research found JAI's team-of-fresh-specialists design wrong for most work: measured studies show such pipelines fail more and cost more, and production systems run one persistent builder with an independent judge. On 8 November about $15 of the $26 day bought nothing (morning MYCO team runs and a duplicate video run), and the BRAG video was split across nine people at medium effort and judged by format tests. JAI now routes work by effort tier, keeps each phase's plan frozen in the project's acceptance ledger, caps each mission's spend, never duplicates a running mission, runs BRAG the way its author intends, and judges creative work by looking at it. The provider cost cross-check was left out at Richard's request (it needs organisation admin keys he does not have).

TASK ID: JAI-UNDERSTANDS-AND-FINISHES
TASK NAME: JAI reads a request by understanding it, its business agents get their full skills, rounds remember their research, the agent goes back to its checklist, and the agent that did the work learns from it
STATUS: COMPLETE
FILES CREATED: apps/backend/app/core/command/understanding.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/steer.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/debrief.py, apps/backend/tests/test_jai_understands_and_finishes.py, apps/backend/tests/test_carry_on_keeps_its_mission.py
FILES MODIFIED: apps/backend/app/core/command/service.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/resume.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/mission_org.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/role_mission.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/agent_executor.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/expertise.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/continuation.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/work_type.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/sandbox_engine.py, apps/backend/app/core/command/tool_runtime.py, apps/backend/app/core/agents/toolbox.py, apps/backend/app/core/agents/workspace_ops.py, apps/backend/app/core/safety/permission_policy.py, apps/backend/app/core/learning/service.py, apps/backend/app/core/config.py, apps/backend/app/main.py
COMMANDS RUN: backend test suite on PostgreSQL (run by the installer); the pure logic of each fix run on Claude's side before packaging
TESTS RUN: Richard's booking follow-up of 14 November is read as more work on the booking mission, filed in Plexecute (not the workspace on screen) and routed to the Partnerships Lead, with that mission's checklist; a reading that names no real mission or is unsure leaves the word lists to decide; a precise instruction keeps its handler; his words reach an agent that is working and stay for its later rounds; the Partnerships Lead is offered prospecting and lead-intelligence and gets a playbook whole; read_skill opens a skill and its reference files and nothing outside the skill's folder; a mission's job and playbooks are the same in later rounds and when carried on; the next round is told what was searched, read and saved; an agent that works 18 steps without marking a requirement is sent back to its checklist; a debriefed lesson is filed under the agent that did the work, used only when its evidence is in the mission's record, and refused by the safety guard when it touches the verifier; workbook facts (rows per sheet, counts per country, rows without a source, duplicates)
RESULT: Audit of 14 November after Richard's typed follow-up ran in the wrong workspace, on another mission's checklist, with the wrong agent. Found and fixed: (1) every decision about a typed request was made by word lists; a model now reads the request with his workspaces, recent missions and the agents in front of it and says what kind of request it is and which mission, workspace and agent it is about (kept on the mission's record and shown in its plan); the word lists are the backup, and the approval gate does not use the model's reading. A follow-up to a mission that is working goes to its agent at its next step and no second mission starts. (2) Playbooks were cut at 7,000 characters and pointed role agents at a tool only the sandbox has; they now arrive whole (24,000), read_skill (authority 1, read-only, logged, in the registry and the permission policy) opens any business skill and its reference files, each trade is offered the skills of the trades next to it, the job's playbooks stay the same across rounds and carry-ons, and start-up logs any registered skill whose file is absent. (3) A new round was told only about files and shell commands; it is now told the searches run, pages read, pages that refused, skills opened and what each workbook save reported; every save and read of a workbook reports its facts, and an agent that stops marking requirements is sent back to its list. (4) Learning was a filled-in sentence filed under the Orchestrator; the role's agent is now debriefed after each round, each lesson names evidence in the mission's record, passes the self-evolution safety guard, and is activated as a versioned SelfEvolutionRecord only when grounded; the role's next mission in that workspace starts with its verified lessons. (5) Role agents are no longer told to verify with commands, and their reports are headed with the role, not "Autonomous engineering run".
BLOCKERS: None. Not yet proved on a live mission: Richard will not run one until this is installed.
NEXT TASK: Install, then carry on mission 0bd089e9 with its own button and check its Verifier result
